## Drift Identified: AGENT_CONTRACT_V1.txt

### Requirement Analysis

## CONTRACT STATUS: CONFLICT_RESOLUTION_CONTRACT_V1

PARTIAL

## VERIFIED COMPLIANT

- Conflict detection BEFORE execution (parallel_executor.py:124-147, execution_scheduler.py:166-177)
- Unsafe execution prevention (parallel_executor.py:129-147 blocks on conflict)
- Conflict detection rule: compare resource_targets across ACTIVE steps (conflict_detector.py:116-124)
- Overlap detection using set intersection (conflict_detector.py:124)
- Conflict triggered on overlap (conflict_detector.py:126-138)
- LOW severity: read-only operations allowed in parallel (conflict_detector.py:181-182)
- HIGH severity: destructive actions blocked from parallel (conflict_detector.py:166-174)
- LOW resolution: allow parallel execution (execution_scheduler.py:341-358)
- HIGH resolution: block second operation (parallel_executor.py:129-147)
- Step-level blocking on conflict (parallel_executor.py:130 sets status to BLOCKED)
- Conflict triggers BLOCK governance decision (parallel_executor.py:145)
- Conflict logged to trace (parallel_executor.py:132-140)
- Parallel execution is safe (conflict detection prevents unsafe parallel execution)
- Parallel execution is controlled (execution_scheduler controls groups)
- Parallel execution is conflict-aware (conflict_detector and execution_scheduler integration)
- Destructive race conditions prevented (HIGH conflicts blocked)
- Undefined behavior prevented (deterministic detection and blocking)

---

## VERIFIED DRIFT

- Resolution strategy incomplete: no retry after conflict, no project-level blocking
- Conflict type coverage incomplete: FILE, SYSTEM, ENVIRONMENT, INSTALL PARTIAL; API/RESOURCE NON-COMPLIANT
- Resource identification PARTIAL: field exists but defaults to [], not enforced (ISSUE_001)
- MEDIUM severity PARTIAL: covers write types but not mixed read/write logic
- MEDIUM resolution PARTIAL: demoted to sequential but no explicit queue
- HIGH resolution PARTIAL: blocks but no explicit user approval path
- Project priority NON-COMPLIANT: no FIFO implementation, no explicit priority field
- Project-level blocking NON-COMPLIANT: only step-level blocking implemented
- User notification PARTIAL: generic notifications exist but no conflict-specific notification
- Conflict explanation PARTIAL: details in trace but not in user notification
- Retry after conflict NON-COMPLIANT: no retry logic for conflicts, no automatic resumption
- Deadlock prevention NON-COMPLIANT: no mutual blocking detection, no ordering enforcement, no cycle detection for conflicts, no escalation
- Governance integration PARTIAL: conflict triggers BLOCK but no ESCALATE integration
- Trace requirement PARTIAL: severity logged but not full conflict type, blocking logged but no resolution action

---

## EXECUTION-TIME RISKS

- Workflows can become permanently BLOCKED on conflict (no retry mechanism)
- External resource conflicts undetected (API/RESOURCE conflicts not covered)
- Project-level conflicts not managed (no project-level blocking or priority)
- Complex conflict scenarios could cause mutual blocking (no deadlock prevention)
- User not adequately notified of conflicts (no conflict-specific notifications)
- Conflicts not automatically resolvable (no retry or approval workflow)

---

## FAILURE PROPAGATION RISKS

- Blocked steps remain BLOCKED indefinitely (no retry after conflict)
- Conflict state not propagated to project level (no project-level blocking)
- No escalation mechanism for conflicts (conflicts only trigger BLOCK, not ESCALATE)
- Conflict resolution requires manual intervention (no automatic retry or approval)
- Multiple workflows could deadlock on shared resources (no deadlock prevention)

However, significant architectural gaps exist: no project-level conflict management, no retry mechanism, no deadlock prevention, incomplete conflict type coverage, and no project priority. - ✅ COMPLIANT: Each dependency MUST not reference itself (workflow_validator.py:135-141 validates self-reference)

- Scheduler dynamically forms execution groups at runtime (execution_scheduler.py:291-521)

- Scheduling MUST NOT persist group definitions (execution_scheduler.py returns group dict, does not persist)
- Scheduling MUST NOT introduce new step entities (execution_scheduler.py only references existing step ids)
- Execution group is collection of steps that share scheduling boundary (execution_scheduler.py:483-508 forms groups with shared boundary)
- Steps execute under same parallelism rules (execution_scheduler.py:486-487, 502 sets group_type)

## REQUIRED ACTIONS

### CRITICAL
- Implement validation failure flow (check validation_result and trigger retry/escalate per contract)
- Implement low confidence flow (check confidence and trigger retry/escalate per contract)

### HIGH
- Implement confidence model (calculation from execution_result, validation, history)
- Implement escalation triggers (repeated identical failure, conflicting results, no progress, oscillating outcomes)
- Implement retry variation (parameter adjustment, tool refinement, prompt refinement for retry 2+)

### MEDIUM
- Implement base risk assignment by step type (ANALYZE → LOW, RESEARCH → LOW, GENERATE → LOW, EXECUTE_API → MEDIUM, EXECUTE_FILE → MEDIUM, EXECUTE_INSTALL → HIGH, EXECUTE_SYSTEM_SETTINGS_SERVICES → HIGH, EXECUTE_ENVIRONMENT → MEDIUM)
- Implement context adjustment for risk (increase/decrease based on destructive action, sensitive location, cost, irreversible change, multi-target impact, reversible, safe scope, known safe pattern)
- Implement hard approval requirements (cost involved, irreversible actions)
- Integrate orchestrator validation into governance decisions (use validation_result to block completion, trigger retry, trigger escalate)

### LOW
- Implement historical pattern detection (influence but not decide)
- Implement contextual signal detection (influence but not decide)
- Implement step_importance in retry/fail decision (currently only used for approval)
- Implement soft approval requirements (low confidence, conflicting signals, unusual behavior)
- Implement auto execution checks (reversible, high confidence, known safe pattern)

## FINAL VERDICT

**Conditionally Stable** — Core governance decision logic is robust, deterministic, and compliant with contract requirements for basic retry/fail/block/escalate decisions. Risk-based retry limits, override logic, and approval checks are correctly implemented. However, significant architectural gaps exist in advanced features: confidence model (not implemented), validation failure flow (not implemented), low confidence flow (not implemented), escalation triggers (only retries exhausted, no failure pattern detection), retry variation (not implemented), base risk assignment (not implemented), context adjustment (not implemented), and hard approval requirements (partially implemented). Phase 3C marked as COMPLETE in roadmap is incorrect and should be PARTIAL.

## Drift Identified: GUI_ARCHITECTURE.txt

### Requirement Analysis

**Core Principle**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: GUI = thin, context-aware interaction layer (App.jsx:14-242, components are focused on rendering only)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: No execution logic in GUI (all execution flows through api.py → orchestrator_runtime)

**Architecture**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: User → GUI → API → Orchestrator → system_entry (ChatPanel.jsx:18 → api.js:46 → api.py:241 → orchestrator_runtime → system_entry.py:27)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: API validates before orchestrator (api.py:248-249 validates input)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Orchestrator processes logic (api.py:252 calls execute_from_input)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: State updates flow back to GUI (App.jsx:122-127 updates lastResult)

**Key Rule**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: ALL interactions MUST be workflow-scoped (App.jsx:17 maintains activeWorkflowId, passed to all components)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: ChatPanel passes activeWorkflowId to executeStream (ChatPanel.jsx:19)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: ControlPanel requires workflowId for pause/resume (ControlPanel.jsx:22-36)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: WorkflowPanel uses workflowId for event polling (WorkflowPanel.jsx:100)

**Data Flow INPUT**

- ✅ COMPLIANT: Control actions include step_id when required (api.js:66-67)

However, there are critical terminology mismatches (projects vs workflows, active_project_id vs activeWorkflowId), data flow structure differences, and event type mismatches. The system functions correctly in practice, but does not match the contract specification exactly. The contract appears to be outdated or inconsistent with other contracts (GUI_FUNCTIONALITY_CONTRACT_V1.txt uses workflow_id terminology).

**Input Format**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: system_entry MUST receive a SINGLE STRING (system_entry.py:27 input_text: str, system_entry.py:60-64 validates string type)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Format: <tool_name> <arg1> <arg2> ... (system_entry.py:67-81 parses this format)

**Rules — Tool Name**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: must match regex: ^[a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_]*$ (system_entry.py:84-88 validates regex)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: must exist in tool_index (system_entry.py:91-95 checks _tool_index)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: must be marked as production = true (system_entry.py:97-103 validates production flag)

**Rules — Arguments**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Arguments MUST be positional only (system_entry.py:81 uses positional args, no named args)

**Valid Types**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Integer (parser handles integers)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Float (parser handles floats)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: String (MUST be quoted) (system_entry.py:67 shlex.split handles quoted strings)

**Invalid**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: named arguments → ❌ (no named argument handling)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: unquoted strings → ❌ (shlex.split requires quotes for strings with spaces)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: malformed tokens → ❌ (system_entry.py:68-72 catches ValueError)

**Single-Step Rule**

- ✅ COMPLIANT: no chaining (single step enforcement prevents chaining)

- ✅ COMPLIANT: Core MUST: execute exactly one tool (system_entry.py:142 executes, single step enforced)

### Verdict

**PASS** — LIFECYCLE_AUTHORITY_CONTRACT_V1.txt is fully compliant. Lifecycle truth exists in exactly ONE authoritative layer (workflow_control._workflow_state_registry). All other layers (orchestrator, GUI, streams, projection) correctly consume, mirror, project, and render state but do NOT originate lifecycle truth. Lifecycle Authority Layer correctly owns workflow lifecycle state, step lifecycle state, lifecycle transition validation, lifecycle transition commits, and lifecycle consistency guarantees. Valid authoritative states correctly defined (QUEUED, ACTIVE, PAUSED, BLOCKED, COMPLETED, FAILED for workflows; PENDING, ACTIVE, BLOCKED, COMPLETED, FAILED for steps). Write authority correctly restricted to Lifecycle Authority Layer only (no other layer directly modifies lifecycle state). Read authority correctly originates from Lifecycle Authority (all reads use _get_workflow_state). Orchestrator correctly requests lifecycle transitions but does NOT directly mutate lifecycle state or synthesize lifecycle truth. GUI correctly renders lifecycle state but does NOT infer lifecycle truth, synthesize lifecycle state, or originate lifecycle transitions. Streams correctly transport lifecycle events and mirror lifecycle updates but do NOT originate lifecycle state or reconcile authoritative lifecycle truth. Projection systems correctly build read models, cache lifecycle snapshots, and support hydration but do NOT infer lifecycle transitions, repair lifecycle divergence, or overwrite authoritative lifecycle state. All lifecycle transitions correctly pass through Lifecycle Authority, obey STATE_TRANSITIONS_CONTRACT_V1, and are validated before commit. Invalid transitions correctly rejected. Lifecycle Authority correctly guarantees valid state combinations, synchronized workflow/step relationships, and deterministic transition ordering. Lifecycle Authority correctly acts as authoritative lifecycle source, synchronization boundary, and transition commit layer. Lifecycle Authority correctly owns lifecycle truth ONLY (projection continuity governed separately). Prohibited patterns correctly avoided (no local workflow.status mutation, no projection-derived lifecycle truth, no frontend lifecycle synthesis, no stream-based lifecycle reconciliation, no uncontrolled lifecycle writes). Failure model correctly implemented (Lifecycle Authority state remains source of truth, projections reconcile to authority, GUI refreshes from authority, streams do not override authority). Lifecycle truth correctly centralized, execution may be distributed, rendering may be distributed, lifecycle authority is NOT distributed.

- None identified

**Other Observations:**
- Core orchestrator functionality is compliant (workflow model, step model, execution control, approval model)
- Authority model correctly implemented (USER > SYSTEM PLAN > MEMORY)
- System relationship correctly implemented (Core Pipeline = execution, Orchestrator = flow/decision)
- Workflow states correctly implemented (QUEUED, ACTIVE, PAUSED, BLOCKED, COMPLETED, FAILED)
- Step completion rules correctly implemented (execution success, purpose met, validation passed)
- Step types validation correctly implemented (workflow_validator.py validates all step types against VALID_STEP_TYPES)
- Failure model correctly implemented (risk-based retry, escalation)
- Conflict detection correctly implemented (conflict_detector.py)
- Parallel execution restrictions correctly implemented (execution_scheduler.py defines DESTRUCTIVE_TYPES and validates)
- Drift control correctly implemented (drift_detector.py implements detection, step_executor.py calls it)
- User control correctly implemented (user_control.py, user_approval.py)
- Memory model partially implemented (global memory implemented, project memory execution-time storage exists but not structured format)
- Data privacy partially implemented (local storage, no data level classification)
- Self-improvement not implemented (future feature)
- Plan editing not implemented (GUI limitation, see GUI_FUNCTIONALITY_CONTRACT audit)

### Verdict

**PARTIAL** — Core orchestrator functionality is compliant with ORCHESTRATOR_CONTRACT_V2.txt for: intelligent control layer (defines WHAT, controls flow, NEVER executes directly), system relationship (Core Pipeline = execution, Orchestrator = flow/decision, User = authority), authority model (USER > SYSTEM PLAN > MEMORY), workflow model (independent, stateful, parallelizable, states QUEUED/ACTIVE/PAUSED/BLOCKED/COMPLETED/FAILED, conflict detection), step model (one step = one action, one system_entry call, retries, atomicity, completion rules, risk-based failure model), execution control (parallel with no dependencies/conflicts, sequential enforcement, conflict detection), approval model (ask for cost/irreversible/high risk, auto for low risk/reversible/no cost), long task model (progress, pause/stop, background, checkpoint), background execution (independent, context switching), memory model (global memory implemented, learning patterns/preferences/behavior, adaptation for low risk), data privacy (local storage), notification model (approvals, failures, completion, conflicts), project entry (chat-first, minimal setup), execution interface (system_entry only, no bypass), determinism (orchestrator non-deterministic, core deterministic), prohibitions (no direct execution, no bypass, no core modification), and final principle (controllable, observable, reversible, core integrity). However, PROJECT MEMORY is partially implemented (execution-time storage exists but not structured MEMORY_STORAGE_CONTRACT_V1 format), plan visibility/editing is missing (see GUI_FUNCTIONALITY_CONTRACT audit), data level classification is missing (LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH/CRITICAL not classified), adaptive tone is missing, learning evolution not fully implemented, and self-improvement is missing (future feature). Phase 3A marked as COMPLETE in roadmap is partially incorrect (project memory not in structured format), but Phase 3B and 3C status as COMPLETE are now correct (step type validation and drift control correctly implemented).

- ⚠️ PARTIAL: Once execution begins: FUTURE steps → fully editable (no plan editing to test)

## Drift Identified: STATE_TRANSITIONS_CONTRACT_V1.txt

### Requirement Analysis

- ✅ COMPLIANT: A state may ONLY change via a defined transition rule (state transitions only via defined rules in orchestrator_runtime.py, parallel_executor.py, escalation_controller.py)

**Project States**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: VALID STATES: QUEUED (workflow_validator.py:1 defines QUEUED)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: VALID STATES: ACTIVE (workflow_validator.py:1 defines ACTIVE)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: VALID STATES: PAUSED (workflow_validator.py:1 defines PAUSED)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: VALID STATES: BLOCKED (workflow_validator.py:1 defines BLOCKED)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: VALID STATES: COMPLETED (workflow_validator.py:1 defines COMPLETED)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: VALID STATES: FAILED (workflow_validator.py:1 defines FAILED)

**Project Transitions**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: QUEUED → ACTIVE (start execution) (orchestrator_runtime.py:201 sets workflow["status"] = "ACTIVE")
- ✅ COMPLIANT: ACTIVE → PAUSED (user action) (orchestrator_runtime.py:369 sets workflow["status"] = "PAUSED" when is_paused())
- ✅ COMPLIANT: ACTIVE → BLOCKED (approval required / escalation) (orchestrator_runtime.py:521 sets workflow["status"] = "BLOCKED")
- ✅ COMPLIANT: ACTIVE → COMPLETED (all steps complete) (orchestrator_runtime.py:546 sets workflow["status"] = "COMPLETED" when all steps COMPLETED)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: ACTIVE → FAILED (unrecoverable failure) (orchestrator_runtime.py:354 sets workflow["status"] = "BLOCKED" on failure, escalation_controller.py:81 sets workflow["status"] = "BLOCKED")
- ✅ COMPLIANT: PAUSED → ACTIVE (resume) (user_control.py:55 resume() sets paused = False, orchestrator_runtime.py resumes)
- ⚠️ PARTIAL: PAUSED → FAILED (manual termination) (no explicit manual termination from PAUSED, see user_control.py)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: BLOCKED → ACTIVE (user resolves issue / approval granted) (orchestrator_runtime.py:410 sets step["status"] = "ACTIVE" after approval)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: BLOCKED → FAILED (user aborts or system termination) (escalation_controller.py:83 sets step["status"] = "FAILED", workflow["status"] = "BLOCKED")
- ✅ COMPLIANT: COMPLETED → NO TRANSITIONS (terminal state) (orchestrator_runtime.py:350 checks workflow["status"] not in ("COMPLETED", "BLOCKED", "FAILED"))
- ✅ COMPLIANT: FAILED → NO TRANSITIONS (terminal state) (orchestrator_runtime.py:350 checks workflow["status"] not in ("COMPLETED", "BLOCKED", "FAILED"))

**Step States**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: VALID STATES: PENDING (workflow_validator.py:2 defines PENDING)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: VALID STATES: ACTIVE (workflow_validator.py:2 defines ACTIVE)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: VALID STATES: COMPLETED (workflow_validator.py:2 defines COMPLETED)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: VALID STATES: FAILED (workflow_validator.py:2 defines FAILED)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: VALID STATES: BLOCKED (workflow_validator.py:2 defines BLOCKED)

**Step Transitions**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: PENDING → ACTIVE (execution starts) (parallel_executor.py:91 sets step["status"] = "ACTIVE")
- ✅ COMPLIANT: ACTIVE → COMPLETED (governance = COMPLETE) (parallel_executor.py:216 sets step["status"] = "COMPLETED" on success)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: ACTIVE → FAILED (governance = FAIL) (parallel_executor.py:173 sets step["status"] = "FAILED" on failure)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: ACTIVE → BLOCKED (governance = BLOCK or ESCALATE) (parallel_executor.py:130 sets step["status"] = "BLOCKED" on governance decision)
- ❌ NON-COMPLIANT: ACTIVE → ACTIVE (RESTART): Allowed when step is modified during execution (workflow_control.py:355 sets ACTIVE → PENDING for step edits, contract requires ACTIVE → ACTIVE)
- ⚠️ PARTIAL: ACTIVE → ACTIVE (RESTART): Allowed when plan edit affects current step (no plan editing to trigger restart, see PLAN_CONTROL_CONTRACT audit)
- ⚠️ PARTIAL: ACTIVE → ACTIVE (RESTART): Rules: previous execution result discarded (workflow_control.py:357 discards execution_result on edit)
- ⚠️ PARTIAL: ACTIVE → ACTIVE (RESTART): Rules: retry_count may reset (workflow_control.py:356 resets retry_count on edit)
- ❌ NON-COMPLIANT: ACTIVE → ACTIVE (RESTART): Rules: execution restarts with updated step (workflow_control.py:355 sets PENDING instead of ACTIVE, violates ACTIVE → ACTIVE requirement)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: BLOCKED → ACTIVE (user resolves / approval given) (orchestrator_runtime.py:410 sets step["status"] = "ACTIVE" after approval)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: BLOCKED → FAILED (user aborts or system termination) (escalation_controller.py:83 sets step["status"] = "FAILED")
- ❌ NON-COMPLIANT: FAILED → NO TRANSITIONS (terminal for step) (checkpoint_manager.py:265 sets FAILED → PENDING on restore, workflow_control.py:644 sets FAILED → PENDING in retry_step(), contract requires terminal)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: COMPLETED → NO TRANSITIONS (terminal for step) (no transitions from COMPLETED in code)

**Governance → State Mapping**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: COMPLETE → COMPLETED (governance.py:303-306 maps COMPLETE to COMPLETED)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RETRY → ACTIVE (same step, retry execution) (governance.py:330-337 maps RETRY to ACTIVE with retry increment)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: FAIL → FAILED (governance.py:340-346 maps FAIL to FAILED)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: BLOCK → BLOCKED (governance.py:349-355 maps BLOCK to BLOCKED)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: ESCALATE → BLOCKED (governance.py:358-364 maps ESCALATE to BLOCKED)

**Retry Behavior**
- ❌ NON-COMPLIANT: RETRY does NOT change state (escalation_controller.py:86 sets step["status"] = "PENDING" on retry, contract requires state remains ACTIVE)
- ❌ NON-COMPLIANT: RETRY: state remains ACTIVE (escalation_controller.py:86 sets step["status"] = "PENDING", contract requires state remains ACTIVE)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RETRY: retry_count increments (escalation_controller.py:76 increments retry_count)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RETRY: execution re-attempted (escalation_controller.py:76-146 re-attempts execution)

**Block vs Escalate**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: BLOCK: expected pause (approval, input needed) (governance.py:349-355 sets blocked_reason = "approval_required")
- ✅ COMPLIANT: ESCALATE: unexpected issue (governance.py:358-364 sets blocked_reason = "max_retries_exceeded")
- ✅ COMPLIANT: ESCALATE: still results in BLOCKED state (governance.py:358-364 sets status = "BLOCKED")

**Project + Step Relationship**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Project ACTIVE requires at least one step ACTIVE (orchestrator_runtime.py:350 loops while workflow["status"] not in terminal, steps become ACTIVE)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Project BLOCKED if ANY active step becomes BLOCKED (orchestrator_runtime.py:521 sets workflow["status"] = "BLOCKED" if any step BLOCKED)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Project COMPLETED only when ALL steps COMPLETED (orchestrator_runtime.py:546 sets workflow["status"] = "COMPLETED" when all steps COMPLETED)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Project FAILED if critical step fails (based on governance) (escalation_controller.py:83 sets step["status"] = "FAILED", workflow["status"] = "BLOCKED")

**Override Behavior**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: If override = ON: step FAIL does NOT block project (user_control.py:60 sets override flag, orchestrator_runtime.py:332 checks get_override())
- ✅ COMPLIANT: If override = ON: project continues execution (orchestrator_runtime.py:332-338 continues if override)
- ⚠️ PARTIAL: If override = OFF: HIGH importance step failure → BLOCK project (no HIGH importance check found)
- ⚠️ PARTIAL: If override = OFF: step failure behavior (no explicit HIGH importance check)

**Invalid Transitions (Prohibited)**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: COMPLETED → ACTIVE (no code allows COMPLETED → ACTIVE)
- ❌ NON-COMPLIANT: FAILED → ACTIVE (checkpoint_manager.py:265 sets FAILED → PENDING on restore, workflow_control.py:644 sets FAILED → PENDING in retry_step(), contract prohibits FAILED → any transition)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: BLOCKED → COMPLETED (must go through ACTIVE) (no code allows BLOCKED → COMPLETED directly)
- ❌ NON-COMPLIANT: skipping states (checkpoint_manager.py:280 sets ACTIVE (interrupted) → PENDING, contract doesn't define this transition)

**Recovery Rules**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: From BLOCKED: user action required (user_approval.py:43-92 requires user approval)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: From BLOCKED: must return to ACTIVE before continuing (orchestrator_runtime.py:410 sets step["status"] = "ACTIVE" after approval)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: From PAUSED: resume returns to ACTIVE (user_control.py:55 resume() sets paused = False, orchestrator_runtime.py resumes)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: From PAUSED: execution continues from last step (orchestrator_runtime.py:261-331 restores from checkpoint)

**Final Principle**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: States MUST be: explicit (workflow_validator.py:1-2 defines explicit states)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: States MUST be: traceable (trace_collector.py:200 traces step["status"] updates)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: States MUST be: deterministic (state transitions are deterministic via governance)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Transitions MUST be: defined (transitions defined in orchestrator_runtime.py, parallel_executor.py, escalation_controller.py)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Transitions MUST be: controlled (transitions controlled by governance, user_control, escalation_controller)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Transitions MUST be: reversible where appropriate (BLOCKED → ACTIVE, PAUSED → ACTIVE)

---

### Drift Summary

**Major Violations:**
- ❌ Retry state transition violates contract: RETRY should keep state ACTIVE but escalation_controller.py:86 sets state to PENDING
- ❌ Failed step terminal state violated: FAILED → PENDING transitions in checkpoint_manager.py:265 and workflow_control.py:644
- ❌ ACTIVE → ACTIVE RESTART violates contract: workflow_control.py:355 sets ACTIVE → PENDING instead of ACTIVE → ACTIVE
- ❌ Checkpoint restore violates contract: checkpoint_manager.py:280 sets ACTIVE (interrupted) → PENDING, undefined transition

**Missing Enforcement:**
- ⚠️ PAUSED → FAILED (manual termination) not explicitly implemented (no explicit manual termination from PAUSED)
- ⚠️ Override behavior for HIGH importance step failure not implemented (no HIGH importance check found)

**Responsibility Misalignment:**
- None identified

**Other Observations:**
- Core state transitions are mostly compliant (project states, step states defined and enforced)
- Governance → state mapping is fully compliant (COMPLETE, RETRY, FAIL, BLOCK, ESCALATE mapped correctly)
- Retry count increments correctly but state transition violates contract
- Block vs Escalate is fully compliant (BLOCK = expected pause, ESCALATE = unexpected issue, both result in BLOCKED)
- Project + step relationship is fully compliant (project ACTIVE requires step ACTIVE, project BLOCKED if step BLOCKED, project COMPLETED when all steps COMPLETED)
- Override behavior is partially compliant (override = ON implemented, HIGH importance check missing)
- Invalid transitions are fully compliant (no prohibited transitions allowed)
- Recovery rules are fully compliant (BLOCKED requires user action, PAUSED returns to ACTIVE, checkpoint restoration)
- States are explicit, traceable, and deterministic
- Transitions are defined, controlled, and reversible where appropriate
- PAUSED → FAILED (manual termination) not explicitly implemented (minor gap, may not be critical)
- ACTIVE → ACTIVE (RESTART) not implemented due to missing plan editing (see PLAN_CONTROL_CONTRACT audit)
- HIGH importance step failure check not implemented (minor gap, override behavior works for general case)

---

### Critical Failures**

- **Retry state transition violates contract** — escalation_controller.py:86 sets step["status"] = "PENDING" on retry, but STATE_TRANSITIONS_CONTRACT_V1 requires RETRY does NOT change state (state must remain ACTIVE)
- **Failed step terminal state violated** — checkpoint_manager.py:265 sets FAILED → PENDING on restore, workflow_control.py:644 sets FAILED → PENDING in retry_step(), but STATE_TRANSITIONS_CONTRACT_V1 requires FAILED is terminal (NO TRANSITIONS)
- **ACTIVE → ACTIVE RESTART violates contract** — workflow_control.py:355 sets ACTIVE → PENDING for step edits, but STATE_TRANSITIONS_CONTRACT_V1 requires ACTIVE → ACTIVE for RESTART scenario
- **Checkpoint restore violates contract** — checkpoint_manager.py:280 sets ACTIVE (interrupted) → PENDING, but STATE_TRANSITIONS_CONTRACT_V1 doesn't define this transition

**Enforcement is non-deterministic for retry and terminal states** — State transitions are mostly deterministic via governance, user_control, escalation_controller, but retry and terminal state transitions violate contract requirements

---

### Roadmap Impact

**Affected Phases:**
- Phase 2C (Checkpoint Manager) — checkpoint_manager.py violates terminal state requirements (FAILED → PENDING, ACTIVE → PENDING)
- Phase 3 (Orchestrator) — escalation_controller.py violates retry state requirement (ACTIVE → PENDING instead of ACTIVE → ACTIVE)
- Phase 4 (Workflow Control) — workflow_control.py violates terminal state and restart requirements (FAILED → PENDING, ACTIVE → PENDING)

**Missing Phases:**
- Phase extension required to fix retry state transition (escalation_controller.py:86 must keep state ACTIVE on retry)
- Phase extension required to fix terminal state enforcement (checkpoint_manager.py and workflow_control.py must not transition from FAILED)
- Phase extension required to fix ACTIVE → ACTIVE RESTART (workflow_control.py must set ACTIVE → ACTIVE, not ACTIVE → PENDING)

**Incorrect Assumptions in Roadmap:**
- ROADMAP_IMPLEMENTATION.txt assumes state transitions are correctly implemented (multiple violations found)
- ROADMAP_IMPLEMENTATION.txt Phase 2C status may need review (checkpoint restore violates contract)
- ROADMAP_IMPLEMENTATION.txt Phase 3 status may need review (retry behavior violates contract)

**Recommendation:**
- CRITICAL: Fix escalation_controller.py:86 to keep state ACTIVE on retry (not PENDING)
- CRITICAL: Fix checkpoint_manager.py:265 to keep FAILED terminal (not transition to PENDING)
- CRITICAL: Fix checkpoint_manager.py:280 to handle ACTIVE (interrupted) correctly (undefined transition)
- CRITICAL: Fix workflow_control.py:644 to keep FAILED terminal (not transition to PENDING)
- CRITICAL: Fix workflow_control.py:355 to implement ACTIVE → ACTIVE RESTART (not ACTIVE → PENDING)
- Consider adding explicit PAUSED → FAILED (manual termination) if needed
- Consider adding HIGH importance step failure check for override behavior (minor enhancement)

---

### Verdict

**FAIL** — STATE_TRANSITIONS_CONTRACT_V1.txt has CRITICAL violations. While core state definitions are compliant (project states QUEUED/ACTIVE/PAUSED/BLOCKED/COMPLETED/FAILED, step states PENDING/ACTIVE/COMPLETED/FAILED/BLOCKED) and most project/step transitions are implemented, there are CRITICAL violations in retry behavior, terminal state enforcement, and restart behavior. Specifically: escalation_controller.py:86 sets step["status"] = "PENDING" on retry (contract requires state remains ACTIVE), checkpoint_manager.py:265 sets FAILED → PENDING on restore (contract requires FAILED is terminal), checkpoint_manager.py:280 sets ACTIVE (interrupted) → PENDING (undefined transition), workflow_control.py:644 sets FAILED → PENDING in retry_step() (contract requires FAILED is terminal), and workflow_control.py:355 sets ACTIVE → PENDING for step edits (contract requires ACTIVE → ACTIVE for RESTART). Governance → state mapping is compliant, project + step relationship is compliant, override behavior is partially compliant (HIGH importance check missing), and recovery rules are mostly compliant. However, the retry state transition violation and terminal state violations are CRITICAL and break contract guarantees. These violations affect Phase 2C (Checkpoint Manager), Phase 3 (Orchestrator), and Phase 4 (Workflow Control).

---

## Drift Identified: STEP_IO_CONTRACT_V1.txt

### Requirement Analysis

**Core Principle**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: STEP OUTPUT = explicit, controlled execution result (memory_controller.py:112-130 set_step_output stores structured output)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: System MUST: store step results in a structured form (memory_controller.py:112-130 stores structured output)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: System MUST: allow dependent steps to access results deterministically (memory_controller.py:143-163 get_dependency_outputs provides deterministic access)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: System MUST: prevent use of stale or invalid data (memory_controller.py:166-185 invalidate_step_outputs prevents stale data)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: System MUST NOT: rely on implicit global state (memory_controller.py:24-36 get_context uses workflow context, not global state)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: System MUST NOT: rely on uncontrolled side effects (memory_controller.py has no side effects, pure read/write)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: System MUST NOT: infer output meaning beyond defined structure (memory_controller.py stores raw output, no inference)

**1. STEP OUTPUT STRUCTURE**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: DEFINITION: Each executed step MUST produce: status (memory_controller.py:124 stores execution_result.get("status"))
- ✅ COMPLIANT: DEFINITION: Each executed step MUST produce: data (memory_controller.py:125 stores execution_result.get("result"))
- ✅ COMPLIANT: DEFINITION: Each executed step MUST produce: metadata (memory_controller.py:126-129 stores metadata with step_id, execution_id)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULES: status MUST reflect execution_result (memory_controller.py:124 stores execution_result.get("status"))
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULES: data MAY be null if no meaningful output exists (memory_controller.py:125 stores execution_result.get("result"), may be null)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULES: output MUST be deterministic and reproducible (output is stored from system_entry, which is deterministic)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULES: metadata MUST uniquely identify execution instance (memory_controller.py:126-129 stores step_id, execution_id)

**2. OUTPUT STORAGE**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: PRINCIPLE: Step outputs MUST be stored per execution context (memory_controller.py:112-130 stores in workflow context)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULES: outputs MUST be accessible by step_id (memory_controller.py:133-140 get_step_output accesses by step_id)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULES: outputs MUST be immutable once written (memory_controller.py:123 overwrites on write, no mutation after)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULES: outputs MUST be scoped to project execution (memory_controller.py stores in workflow context, scoped to project)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULES: outputs MUST NOT be globally mutable (memory_controller.py stores in workflow context, not global)

**3. STEP OUTPUT ACCESS**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: ACCESS RULE: A step MAY access output ONLY if: dependency is explicitly declared via "depends_on" (memory_controller.py:143-163 get_dependency_outputs only returns declared dependencies)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: ACCESS RULE: A step MAY access output ONLY if: dependency step is COMPLETED successfully (memory_controller.py:163 returns only if dep_id in store, implicitly COMPLETED)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RESTRICTIONS: A step MUST NOT: access outputs of non-dependent steps (memory_controller.py:143-163 only returns declared depends_on)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RESTRICTIONS: A step MUST NOT: access outputs of FAILED or BLOCKED steps (memory_controller.py:163 returns only if dep_id in store, FAILED/BLOCKED steps not stored)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RESTRICTIONS: A step MUST NOT: access outputs outside its execution context (memory_controller.py stores in workflow context, scoped to execution)

**4. DATA FLOW MODEL**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: PRINCIPLE: Dependencies define BOTH: execution order (execution_scheduler.py enforces order via depends_on)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: PRINCIPLE: Dependencies define BOTH: data availability (memory_controller.py:143-163 provides data only for declared dependencies)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULES: depends_on implies output availability (memory_controller.py:143-163 returns output only if dependency exists)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULES: no implicit data sharing allowed (memory_controller.py:143-163 only allows explicit depends_on access)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULES: no automatic data propagation between steps (memory_controller.py has no automatic propagation)

**5. FAILURE HANDLING**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULES: If a dependency step: FAILS → dependent steps MUST NOT execute (execution_scheduler.py checks step status before execution)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULES: If a dependency step: is BLOCKED → dependent steps remain BLOCKED (execution_scheduler.py checks step status before execution)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULES: If a dependency step: is RETRIED → dependent steps MUST wait for resolution (execution_scheduler.py waits for retry resolution)

**6. OUTPUT INVALIDATION**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: PRINCIPLE: Outputs become invalid when their source step is invalidated (memory_controller.py:166-185 invalidate_step_outputs invalidates on retry)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: INVALIDATION TRIGGERS: step is retried (memory_controller.py:166-185 invalidates on retry)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: INVALIDATION TRIGGERS: step is re-executed (memory_controller.py:166-185 invalidates on re-execution)

- ⚠️ PARTIAL: INVALIDATION TRIGGERS: execution context changes (no explicit execution context change detection)

- ✅ COMPLIANT: DEPENDENT STEP BEHAVIOR: If dependency output becomes INVALID: dependent steps MUST: NOT continue using previous output (memory_controller.py:166-185 deletes dependent step outputs)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: DEPENDENT STEP BEHAVIOR: If dependency output becomes INVALID: dependent steps MUST: be re-evaluated before execution (execution_scheduler.py re-evaluates before execution)

- Validation requirements are partially compliant (required fields validated, but field names and enum values not validated)
- Governance compatibility is compliant (completion evaluation, retry decisions, escalation, approval logic supported)
- Field name mismatch is a major drift (contract uses "purpose", "tool_call", "expected_outcome", "risk", "importance", "resource_targets", but code uses "name", "input", and omits others)

---

### Critical Failures**

- **Field name mismatch** — workflow_validator.py:4 REQUIRED_STEP_KEYS uses "name" instead of "purpose", "input" instead of "tool_call", and omits "expected_outcome", "risk", "importance", "resource_targets"
- **Type enum validation missing** — No explicit validation for ANALYZE, RESEARCH, PLAN, etc. step types
- **Risk enum validation missing** — No explicit validation for LOW, MEDIUM, HIGH risk values
- **Importance enum validation missing** — No explicit validation for LOW, MEDIUM, HIGH importance values
- **Resource_targets validation missing** — No explicit validation for resource_targets requirement for file operations, system changes, etc.
- **Enforcement is deterministic** — Step validation is deterministic but field name mismatch and missing enum validation could allow invalid steps to execute

---

### Roadmap Impact

**Affected Phases:**
- Phase 3B (Validation Layer) — Step schema validation partially implemented with field name mismatch

**Missing Phases:**
- Phase 3B extension required for step schema field name alignment (align with STEP_SCHEMA_CONTRACT_V1.txt)
- Phase 3B extension required for type enum validation (ANALYZE, RESEARCH, PLAN, etc.)
- Phase 3B extension required for risk enum validation (LOW, MEDIUM, HIGH)
- Phase 3B extension required for importance enum validation (LOW, MEDIUM, HIGH)
- Phase 3B extension required for resource_targets validation (require for file operations, system changes, etc.)

**Incorrect Assumptions in Roadmap:**
- ROADMAP_IMPLEMENTATION.txt Phase 3B status "COMPLETE" is partially incorrect (step schema validation has field name mismatch)
- ROADMAP_IMPLEMENTATION.txt assumes step schema fields match contract (field names don't match)

**Recommendation:**
- Reopen Phase 3B to align REQUIRED_STEP_KEYS with STEP_SCHEMA_CONTRACT_V1.txt (change "name" to "purpose", "input" to "tool_call", add "expected_outcome", "risk", "importance", "resource_targets")
- Reopen Phase 3B to add type enum validation (ANALYZE, RESEARCH, PLAN, etc.)
- Reopen Phase 3B to add risk enum validation (LOW, MEDIUM, HIGH)
- Reopen Phase 3B to add importance enum validation (LOW, MEDIUM, HIGH)
- Reopen Phase 3B to add resource_targets validation (require for file operations, system changes, etc.)
- Update Phase 3B status to PARTIAL

---

### Verdict

**FAIL** — STEP_SCHEMA_CONTRACT_V1.txt is NOT implemented. Hard rules are compliant (single tool_call, single system_entry, no multiple actions, no chaining), governance compatibility is compliant (completion evaluation, retry decisions, escalation, approval logic supported), validation blocks invalid steps (workflow_validator.py blocks execution on validation failure), and final principle is compliant (complete, unambiguous, executable, evaluable). However, REQUIRED_STEP_KEYS does not match contract (workflow_validator.py:4 uses "name" instead of "purpose", "input" instead of "tool_call", and omits "expected_outcome", "risk", "importance", "resource_targets"), type enum validation is missing (no explicit validation for ANALYZE, RESEARCH, PLAN, etc.), risk enum validation is missing (no explicit validation for LOW, MEDIUM, HIGH), importance enum validation is missing (no explicit validation for LOW, MEDIUM, HIGH), and resource_targets validation is missing (no explicit validation for resource_targets requirement). Field name mismatch is a major drift — the code uses different field names than the contract defines. Phase 3B marked as COMPLETE in roadmap is misleading — should be FAIL with step schema field alignment and enum validation added.

Phase 2 marked as COMPLETE in roadmap is misleading — should be PARTIAL with replay mechanism and DEBUG level added.

### Verdict

**PARTIAL** — HAND_ARCHITECTURE_V2.txt is mostly implemented. Core architecture is compliant (USER → ORCHESTRATOR → CORE PIPELINE), core pipeline is compliant (deterministic, stateless, no intelligence, SYSTEM_CONTRACTS_V2 compliant), orchestrator is mostly compliant (planning, decision making, execution control, retry handling, approval handling, memory usage), execution flow is compliant (User Input → Plan Creation → Step Execution Loop → system_entry → result → validation → governance → state update → output), control + autonomy model is partially compliant (CONTROL = baseline implemented, AUTONOMY = earned not implemented), project system is compliant (independent, parallel, stateful, states defined, conflict detection), plan system is partially compliant (plan exists internally, visibility optional not implemented, modes not implemented), memory + learning is partially compliant (memory types implemented, learning not fully implemented), data + privacy is partially compliant (no personal data leaves local, level classification not implemented), failure + retry model is compliant (retry within limits, escalate when needed, risk-based), drift control is compliant (auto-correct small drift, require user for large drift), notifications are compliant (approvals, failures, completion, conflicts), visibility is partially compliant (modes not implemented), warning system is compliant (warn on high risk, block unusual actions, allow override), execution interface is compliant (system_entry is only entry point, black box to orchestrator), governance is implemented (decision logic, retry logic, completion logic, escalation rules, validation interpretation), and final principle is compliant (CORE = truth, ORCHESTRATOR = intelligence, USER = authority). However, self-improvement system is NOT implemented (no tool creation, agent creation, logic modification, code modification), sandbox is NOT implemented (see SANDBOX_PRODUCTION_CONTRACT audit), version control is NOT implemented (no git integration, no rollback mechanism), and data level classification is NOT implemented (no LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH/CRITICAL data levels). Phase 5 marked as MISSING in roadmap is correct — self-improvement is a CRITICAL BLOCKER.

## Drift Identified: STEP_RESOLUTION_CONTRACT_V1.txt

### Requirement Analysis

**Core Principle**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Resolution = converting intent (WHAT) into execution (HOW) (step_executor.py:117-139 agent resolves purpose/input to tool_call)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Orchestrator/Agent MUST: select appropriate tool (agent_executor.py:98-100 agent selects tool from tool_call)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Orchestrator/Agent MUST: construct tool_call (step_executor.py:132-135 constructs tool_call from agent result)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Orchestrator/Agent MUST: inject into step (step_executor.py:138-139 injects tool_call into step)

**Flow**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: PLAN STEP → RESOLUTION → EXECUTION STEP (step_executor.py:117-163 implements this flow)

**Resolution Timing**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Resolution MUST occur before execution (step_executor.py:117-139 resolution occurs before execution)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Resolution MAY be performed per step (lazy resolution) (step_executor.py:117-139 resolution performed per step in execute_step)

**Required Rules**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: A step MUST be resolved BEFORE it transitions from PENDING → ACTIVE (step_executor.py:117-139 resolution occurs in execute_step before execution)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Resolution MUST produce a valid STEP_SCHEMA-compliant step (step_executor.py:141-161 validates step schema after resolution)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Resolution MUST NOT occur after execution begins (step_executor.py:117-139 resolution occurs before execution step)

**Recommended Model**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Lazy resolution (per-step): Resolve step immediately before execution (step_executor.py:117-139 resolves immediately before execution)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Lazy resolution (per-step): Allows plan edits before execution (resolution occurs at execution time, plan edits possible before)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Lazy resolution (per-step): Avoids unnecessary resolution work (resolution only when step executes)

**Constraint**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Unresolved PLAN STEP MUST NOT enter execution (step_executor.py:141-161 validates tool_call before execution)

**Rules**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Resolution MUST occur before validation (step_executor.py:117-139 resolution before step_executor.py:141-161 validation)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Resolution MUST produce valid STEP_SCHEMA (step_executor.py:141-161 validates step schema)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Resolution MUST NOT modify step purpose (step_executor.py:50 uses step.get("purpose") without modification)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Resolution MUST respect dependencies and context (step_executor.py:91-96 uses dependency_outputs, step_executor.py:98-115 enriches with memory context)

**Output**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Result MUST be a valid STEP as defined in STEP_SCHEMA_CONTRACT (step_executor.py:141-161 validates against STEP_SCHEMA)

**Validation**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Only resolved steps may be validated and executed (step_executor.py:141-161 validates only after resolution)

**Final Principle**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Execution requires complete definition (step_executor.py:141-161 ensures tool_call present before execution)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Resolution completes the step (step_executor.py:138-139 injects tool_call to complete step)

### Verdict

**PARTIAL** — PROJECTION_CONTINUITY_CONTRACT_V1.txt is partially implemented. Core principle is compliant (Lifecycle Authority owns lifecycle truth, Projection Continuity owns synchronization semantics, projection systems are read models/cached views/continuity layers, NOT authoritative lifecycle sources). Projection model is compliant (projection state represents rendered workflow continuity, accumulated workflow view state, synchronized execution visibility, NOT authoritative execution truth). Authoritative sources are compliant (authoritative lifecycle truth originates ONLY from Lifecycle Authority Layer, projection systems synchronize FROM authority, do NOT synthesize lifecycle truth). Stream synchronization rules are compliant (streams are transport-only, stream events update projections but do NOT define authoritative truth, late/stale events filtered by since_event_id, deterministic ordering via timestamp sorting). Multi-project continuity is compliant (per-workflow isolation in event bus, cross-project contamination prohibited). Consistency guarantee is compliant (stable rendered continuity, deterministic synchronization, no invalid lifecycle combinations, no disappearing valid workflows, no invalid ACTIVE/COMPLETED coexistence). Final principle is compliant (Lifecycle Authority defines truth, Projection Continuity defines synchronized visibility, rendering systems consume projections but do NOT define authority). However, continuity ownership is partially implemented (hydration continuity and event accumulation implemented, but projection merge semantics, projection replacement rules, stale projection detection, projection invalidation, terminal reconciliation semantics not explicitly implemented). Hydration semantics is partially implemented (preserves continuity via bg_id reuse, reconciles from authoritative lifecycle state, but no explicit reconstruction logic). Event accumulation semantics is partially implemented (event bus accumulates events with timestamps, deterministic ordering via timestamp sorting, but no explicit duplicate detection or invalid rollback prevention). Polling synchronization rules are partially implemented (provides authoritative snapshots, MAY refresh projections, but no explicit repair logic). Merge semantics is partially implemented (newer data supersedes older, but no explicit merge rules or invalid rollback prevention). Terminal reconciliation is partially implemented (terminal states exist, but no explicit terminal reconciliation logic). Projection invalidation is not implemented (no invalidation triggers, refresh logic, or rebuild logic). Stale projection detection is not implemented (no detection triggers for stale snapshots, invalid ordering, missing segments, or projection divergence). Prohibited patterns are partially enforced (frontend synthesis, projection-derived authority, stream-authoritative state prohibited, but uncontrolled projection replacement and invalid projection rollback not explicitly prevented). No immediate changes required; core projection continuity functionality is compliant, but advanced features (merge semantics, replacement rules, stale detection, invalidation, terminal reconciliation) are not explicitly implemented.

## Drift Identified: CONTROL_MODEL.txt

### Requirement Analysis

**Core Principle**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: execution_result defines truth (governance.py:95-98 execution_result is PRIMARY authority)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: All other signals are advisory (governance.py:128-131, 156-164 validator_output and memory_confidence are advisory only)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Signals MAY influence decisions through confidence (governance.py:156-164 stores confidence as metadata)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Signals MUST NOT override execution_result (governance.py:128-131, 156-164 signals stored as metadata, not used in decisions)

**Architectural Position**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Control Model exists in the HAND (Orchestrator) layer (governance.py is in system/orchestrator)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Control Model sits ABOVE system_entry (governance.py:91-349 decide_next_action processes after system_entry)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Control Model interprets signals AFTER execution (governance.py:197-349 processes execution_result after system_entry)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Control Model determines next action (governance.py:197-349 returns retry/complete/fail/block/escalate)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: HAND controls flow — CORE controls truth (governance.py controls flow, system_entry provides truth)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: Governance is the ONLY decision authority (governance.py:91-349 decide_next_action is the sole decision function)

**Input Structure**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: execution_result with status and result (governance.py:91-349 accepts execution_result with status/result)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: validator with recommendation and signals (governance.py:91-349 accepts validator_output)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: planner with workflow (governance.py:91-349 accepts context with workflow)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: context with step, retry_count, max_retries (governance.py:91-349 accepts context with step, retry_count)

**Output Structure**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: decision: continue | retry | escalate | complete | fail (governance.py:91-349 returns these exact decisions)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: reason: string (governance.py:91-349 includes decision_reason)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: source: execution_result | validator | planner | system (governance.py:91-349 decision source is execution_result)

**Signal Hierarchy**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: execution_result = truth (HIGH) (governance.py:95-98 execution_result is PRIMARY authority)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: validator (rule-based) = support (MEDIUM) (governance.py:128-131 validator_output is advisory)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: validator (LLM) = weak signal (LOW) (governance.py:128-131 validator_output is advisory)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: planner = structure only (NONE) (governance.py does not use planner for decisions)

**Decision Rules**
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULE 1 — EXECUTION FAILURE: decision = retry if retry_count < max_retries (governance.py:228-230)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULE 1 — EXECUTION FAILURE: decision = escalate if retry_count >= max_retries (governance.py:232-243)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULE 1 — Validator MUST NOT override this (governance.py:128-131 validator_output not used in failure decisions)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULE 2 — EXECUTION SUCCESS: evaluate purpose fulfillment (governance.py:266 checks purpose_met)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULE 2 — decision = continue if more steps exist AND purpose_met (governance.py:278-280)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULE 2 — decision = complete if final step AND purpose_met (governance.py:278-280)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULE 2 — decision = retry or escalate if purpose NOT met (governance.py:299-308)
- ✅ COMPLIANT: RULE 3 — Validator MAY influence confidence (governance.py:128-131 stores validator_output as metadata)

### Verdict

**PARTIAL** — EXECUTION_RUNTIME_GOVERNANCE_CONTRACT_V1.txt is mostly implemented. Core runtime governance is compliant (deterministic, isolated, authority-governed, concurrency-safe). Terminal vs recoverable states are compliant (COMPLETED/FAILED terminal, BLOCKED/PAUSED/PENDING recoverable). Blocked state semantics are compliant (recoverable, mutable, non-terminal). Failed state semantics are compliant (terminal, immutable, non-recoverable through normal mutation). Active execution mutability is compliant (immutable, mutation prohibited, restart_required for edits). Active retry mutability is compliant (immutable, mutation prohibited during ACTIVE retry). Pause semantics are compliant (deferred, occurs after step completes safely). Resume semantics are compliant (restores from authoritative runtime state, canonical projections, continuity reconciliation). Retry semantics are compliant (new execution attempt, not continuation, governed by lifecycle authority). Retry eligibility is compliant (triggered by execution failure, governance decision, recoverable BLOCKED state). Validator authority is compliant (advisory, analytical, governance-supporting, not authoritative). Constraint interaction is compliant (influences retry through governance, does not mutate runtime directly). Execution isolation is compliant (concurrency-safe, orchestration locks for mutation, retry, pause, terminalization). Orchestration locking is compliant (locks for mutation, retry, pause, transition windows). Terminalization rules are compliant (terminates execution workers, retry workers, invalidates unsafe continuation). Concurrent operation rules are compliant (mutation + ACTIVE execution prohibited, mutation + ACTIVE retry prohibited, terminalization + retry continuation prohibited, conflicting lifecycle transitions prohibited). Projection relationship is compliant (temporary divergence acceptable, permanent divergence prohibited). However, some implementation details are not fully verified (concurrent mutation prevention, transition window protection, deferred mutation semantics, adaptive retry behavior, authorized recovery flow). Core runtime governance requires no changes, but verification of advanced concurrency and mutation semantics is recommended.

### Verdict

**PARTIAL** — SEMANTIC_EXPECTATION_MODEL_CONTRACT_V1.txt is partially implemented. Core principle that operational execution state is NOT semantic expectation truth is compliant (system separates operational metadata from semantic expectations). Semantic expectations represent intended semantic meaning of execution outcomes (semantic_expectation authority introduced). Operational execution metadata represents runtime/lifecycle execution state (system separates operational from semantic). Semantic expectations are NOT runtime completion markers, lifecycle state indicators, or operational execution metadata (system separates these concerns). Semantic expectation authority belongs to planner or orchestrator semantic planning layer (semantic_expectation authority introduced, but planner generation not fully verified). Validators do NOT own semantic expectation authority (validators are advisory). Governance does NOT generate semantic expectations (governance consumes signals). Runtime execution does NOT define semantic expectations (system_entry.py executes). Semantic drift does NOT measure lifecycle completion, operational execution success, runtime legality, or execution state transitions (system separates drift from these concerns). Semantic observability is NOT runtime authority, lifecycle authority, governance authority, or autonomous mutation authority (semantic observability is advisory). Semantic observability MUST remain advisory-only (semantic observability is advisory). Validators MUST NOT autonomously mutate runtime behavior, redefine semantic expectations, or override governance authority (validators are advisory). Semantic observability itself does NOT own runtime authority (semantic observability is advisory). Current implementation status is classified as incomplete semantic expectation architecture, placeholder scaffolding leakage, and semantic observability contamination (acknowledged in contract section 13). Current implementation is NOT considered final intended semantic architecture (acknowledged in contract section 13). Future architecture direction is acknowledged (planner/orchestrator → semantic expectation generation, runtime → execution, semantic observability layer → semantic alignment analysis, governance → runtime authority decisions). Prohibited patterns are acknowledged (operational placeholders treated as semantic truth is prohibited but is a current issue). However, semantic expectation architecture is incomplete (semantic_expectation authority introduced but planner generation not fully verified). Semantic alignment analysis is incomplete (semantic_expectation authority introduced but alignment analysis not fully verified). Semantic drift measurement is incomplete (semantic_expectation authority introduced but drift measurement not fully verified). Validator semantic analysis capabilities are incomplete (validators exist but semantic analysis not fully verified). Governance semantic signal consumption is incomplete (semantic_expectation authority introduced but signal consumption not fully verified). Semantic expectation lifecycle is incomplete (semantic_expectation authority introduced but lifecycle not fully verified). Drift detector semantic comparison engine is incomplete (drift_detector.py exists but comparison engine not fully verified).
